Black king is much closer to the pawns.

White king h7 -> g7 -> f7 -> e7 -> d7 -> c7 -> b7 -> a7.

c5d4: doesn't seem better.

White King at a1: can it move to b1? Maybe. Can it move to b2? No, that's controlled by the king at c3 (if it stays) or c2.

Is a2 attacked by K at b3? Yes.
Is b2 attacked by K at b3? Yes.
So if Black King reaches b3, and the pawn stays at a3, it's checkmate?
Wait, for the king at a1 to be in check, it must be attacked by a piece. The king at b3 does not attack a1.
To checkmate the king at a1, we need a piece to attack a1.
The pawn at a3 does not attack a1.
The king at b3 does not attack a1.
So it's not checkmate, it's stalemate if the king can't move.

But wait, if the King is at b3, the white king at a1 has no legal moves?
- a2: occupied.
- b1: attacked by K b3.
- b2: attacked by K b3 and P a3.
Yes, if black king is at b3, white king has no moves. This would be stalemate unless white has other pieces.
But white has a pawn at a2. Is the pawn blocked?
Yes, the black pawn is at a3.
So if black moves to b3, it's stalemate.

Wait, let's re-evaluate.
Current board:
